# Project-wide P versus NP audit

## The fiber/avoidance spine, an exact vector construction, and the strongest surviving proof route

**Research update, 24 September 2026:** Proposition 18.9 proves an overlap-independent $O(\log M)$ cover for any selected family of $M$ uniform-majority filters with support size at least three, and for families whose collision mass is at most $1/48$. Thus the one-uniform-witness-per-anchor route is below the magnification scale when the anchor count is $2^{O(N^\beta)}$ for fixed $\beta<1$. The result is limited to majority-measure filters; Idea 57 gives a four-point example showing why it does not extend to every semi-filter by simply assigning additive weights.

**Research update, 25 September 2026:** Sections 97-99 audit Buono's June 2026 witness-complexity preprint. Under its stated definition, the class-relative measure is exactly $\Theta(|x|)$ because per-object polynomial-time machines may hardcode outputs; the claimed superpolynomial family and the proof of the reverse characterization do not follow. Its uniform rich-witness conjecture is impossible on simple SAT inputs by $K(f(w))\leq K(w)+O(1)$. A derived random-padding lemma shows that a P solver on the high-K slice of padded SAT would imply $NP\subseteq BPP$ (and $P/poly$), not $P=NP$. These are verified audits and class-containment consequences, not a separation.

**Date:** 24 August 2026  
**Status:** No proof of either \(P=NP\) or \(P\ne NP\) was found. The Clay Mathematics Institute still lists the problem as unsolved.  
**Additional research update, 24 September 2026:** Propositions 18.10-18.26 close several natural constructions and introduce fusion-susceptibility measures. Proposition 18.17 proves every nonprincipal semi-filter has a two-coordinate OR face, with coordinate-dependent defect exactly $1/8$; Proposition 18.19 gives an exact quadratic lower bound for an unrestricted co-singleton family. The co-singleton embedding into anchored Gap-MCSP remains open. Proposition 18.20 proves a two-pair collapse for dense two-cylinder filters whenever both coordinate blocks exceed the logarithm of the excluded promise set. Proposition 18.21 reduces exact cover search for two-minimum filters to $3^M$ witness orientations. Proposition 18.22 gives an exact two-rectangle formula for cylinder-filter coverage and explains why ordinary communication rectangles may overcover non-anchor patterns. Proposition 18.23 gives a distance-sensitive $O(N^{2-\beta}\log^2 N)$ cover for two-minimum anchored filters in the Gap-MCSP regime. Proposition 18.24 embeds an affine-grid incidence design into the actual dense Gap-MCSP promise and proves that it has a one-pair cover, even with every literal generator included. Proposition 18.25 gives an $O(d)$ cover (at most $d+1$ pairs) for affine line-pencil filters in projected dimension $d$. Proposition 18.26 extends the blocker argument to all affine $r$-flat pencils, with at most $d-r+2$ pairs; a $3$-dimensional $\mathbb F_4$ toy verifies the three-pair case for affine plane pencils. Exhaustive and exact finite tests of sparse one-witness families now include a 16-anchor constant-weight toy: random families need at most two pairs, and a sample-guided minimax construction (then checked over all endpoint pairs) has exact cover two with maximum single-pair coverage $13/16$. A structured seven-anchor shell extension also has exact cover two after checking all $2^{30}$ ordered endpoint pairs. The counterexample-guided SAT search for a three-pair witness assignment was interrupted on 25 September at 190 iterations and 2,972 blocking clauses; it had not produced a construction or impossibility certificate. These are construction-specific closures and finite results. Arbitrary endpoint fusion remains the obstacle; no P-versus-NP separation is proved.
**Finite incidence update, 24 September 2026:** The Fano-plane filter pilot has exact fusion cover three, but the analogous $PG(2,3)$ family has cover two; an ordinary weak-coloring explanation was falsified by a five-vertex counterexample with overlap contamination. Idea 79 records a fusion-spectrum formulation, a finite fractional-cover integrality gap, and a witness-union normal form. The sparse-witness CEGIS run was stopped on 25 September at its last verified checkpoint of 190 iterations and 2,972 clauses; this supplies no result beyond that checkpoint. These are finite structural results; no P-vs-NP proof has been found.

**Graph-incidence update, 24 September 2026:** For filters generated by graph edges incident to each anchor vertex, every fusion profile is an independent set with a two-sided neighborhood-splitting condition. This gives an exact graph characterization and a chromatic lower bound. On $C_6$, the ordinary chromatic number is two but the exact fusion cover is three. The graph model's anchors and universe have the same size, so this does not meet the project's superlinear target; a coded-anchor lift must still control literal-generator bypasses.

The first direct literal-overlay test closes the naive $C_6$ lift on a toy: all 25 tested anchor-to-shell injections had an exact one-pair cover. In the canonical assignment, literals alone cover four anchors and graph witnesses cover the other two. A 16-anchor $K_4$-core coded lift has cover at most two in all 30 exact assignments; the canonical winning pair uses literals on 15 anchors and one graph witness to repair the last. Mixed literal/edge profiles are the concrete bypass that the lift must prevent.

The distance-three coded follow-up uses eight punctured-simplex anchors and a ten-point weight-two universe. All 100 sampled graph-token assignments have a one-pair cover after exhaustive endpoint-pair enumeration, and the first assignment already has a literal-only cover-one pair. Graph witnesses block three anchors in that particular pair's intersection, but another mixed pair restores all eight. Idea 81 then identifies the product-domain obstruction exactly: every full-product coordinate-slice family, over any alphabet of size at least two and with at least two coordinates, has a two-pair checkerboard cover. Thus code distance and graph independence do not control the fusion spectrum while coordinate slices remain available.

Idea 82 corrects the scope of that profile calculation: those two profiles cover the minimal coordinate-generated filters, not every semi-filter above each anchor. On the disjoint punctured 4-cube with anchors $0000,1111$, exact closure finds a preserving semi-filter for both anchors under the checkerboard pair list. A separate sparse 3-cube with anchors $000,111$ has exact universal cover two: all 2,080 one-pair candidates were ruled out and an explicit two-pair list forces closure to empty for both anchors. The closure is an anchor-indexed Horn system: each endpoint pair supplies an intersection rule, and the forced slices are its initial facts. A genuine lower bound on the project's cover measure must reason about the universal semi-filter quantifier, not just a selected family of filters.

**Closure-language update:** The universal semi-filter cover is an anchor-indexed Horn refutation system: each endpoint pair supplies an intersection rule and the forced coordinate slices are initial facts. A lower bound must show that every short rule list leaves some anchor's least fixed point nonempty.

**Relay update, 24 September 2026:** Idea 83 derives an exact universal-cover value for the punctured Boolean cube $U_n=\{0,1\}^n\setminus\{0^n,1^n\}$ with anchors $0^n,1^n$: the semi-filter cover number is exactly $n-1$. The upper bound uses endpoint sets $B_i=\{x:x_i=0\text{ or }x_{i+1}=1\}$ around a directed cycle; the lower bound is the proof-DAG leaf argument. The checker verifies the explicit relay for $n=2$ through $12$. The cyclic computation viewpoint is already covered by Cavalar and Oliveira's exact characterization $\rho=D_{\cap}^{\circ}$ (Theorem 30) and their fixed-rule closure/cyclic-circuit example (Section 2.5); this work is an explicit toy instance, not a new general framework. It does not transfer to Gap-MCSP.

**Anchor-sharing update, 24 September 2026:** For anchors of weight at most one and universe strings of weight at least two, exact SAT searches give cover values $2,4,6$ for dimensions $n=3,4,5$. The five-rule n=5 formula is UNSAT under audited endpoint, rule, and coordinate symmetry breaking; the verified six-rule witness therefore gives computed exact value six (no DRAT trace was saved). Different anchors use different activation schedules. Idea 84 records the toy and its exact finite results; Idea 85 gives a general $2n-4$-rule construction, so this family is only linear and cannot meet the magnification target.

**Threshold-state update, 24 September 2026:** A prefix-count recurrence builds the empty set for every weight-at-most-one anchor using $2n-4$ universal relay rules, verified by exact support closure for $n=3$ through $12$. A single-anchor proof-DAG argument gives $n-2\le\rho$, so the toy family has $\Theta(n)$ cover complexity. The construction is a threshold-function dynamic program already within classical monotone circuit theory; the remaining mathematical question is whether cyclic covers can improve the coefficient asymptotically. Neither this result nor the finite exact values transfer to Gap-MCSP.

**Sorting-network update, 24 September 2026:** A bitonic sorting network on the mandatory zero-slice generators gives a universal $O(n\log^2 n)$ relay cover for every symmetric threshold toy $A_{n,k}=\{a:\operatorname{wt}(a)\le k\}$, uniformly in $k$. The explicit verifier checks all thresholds through $n=8$. This closes the whole symmetric threshold family as a source of an $n^{1+\varepsilon}$ cover lower bound. The sorting network is classical; the new project result is its exact translation into the all-semi-filter preservation closure. See Section 43 and Idea 87.

**Failure-synthesis update, 24 September 2026:** The common missing term across the closure experiments is cross-anchor proof reuse. One anchor's empty derivation requires $N-o(N)$ matching literal leaves, yielding the near-linear lower bound in Proposition 21.7; the leaf argument does not charge reuse across anchors. Exact traces of the n=4 four-rule and n=5 six-rule threshold covers show that every listed rule fires for every anchor, so raw rule-support size is not the missing invariant. A new exact context-antichain verifier checks every subset of initial generators and records anchor-specific minimal leaf patterns for each carrier on these examples. Idea 88 refines the target to contextual proof reuse: how many distinct leaf patterns can pass through a shared relay sub-DAG without retaining a common high-table witness? This is a research target, not an established lower bound.

**Basis-scrambling update, 24 September 2026:** A proposed route applies a fast invertible linear transform to truth-table coordinates, turning the mandatory literal slices into parity hyperplanes while preserving circuit lower bounds up to transform cost. On the 3-bit weight-at-most-one toy, all 168 matrices in $GL(3,2)$ give cover at most two. In dimension four, the exhaustive orbit audit of 840 unordered bases finds 14 classes: solver first-SAT counts are 2 for one class, 3 for three, 4 for nine, and 5 for one. The exceptional five-rule witness is exact-closure checked; UNSAT lower searches lack proof traces. This is a new finite positive cover result but only a constant toy improvement, with no high-dimensional transfer.

**Scope:** This memo audits the project artifacts and local ChatGPT-project notes available in this workspace and mirrors, corrects earlier overclaims, connects the work to primary literature, and pushes the combined ideas as far as possible without crossing from theorem into conjecture. It does not claim access to unexported conversations or a complete census of all P-versus-NP literature.

**Research update, 23 September 2026:** Follow-up tests in Section 12 eliminate the unrestricted graph proxy, canonical bit-literal filters, high-entropy majority measures, constant-support majority measures, the tested one-heavy-atom mixture, and existential majority-support filters as routes to the required superlinear cover lower bound. A recent Komlos vector-balancing theorem gives an additional simultaneous-cover criterion (Proposition 12.10), which further narrows the measure regime a cover lower bound would need to exploit. Section 18 derives a five-piece aggregate-load criterion with threshold $\delta<1/468$, the uniform-support condition $t>468\sqrt d$, and an exact support-overlap-graph bound of $\chi(G)$ pairs (at most $t(d-1)+1$ under point overlap $d$). Corollary 18.8 shows that one sparse majority witness per anchor can be selected disjointly from the large high-complexity spheres, making that natural family coverable by one pair even at logarithmic support sizes. New September discrepancy literature also gives a deterministic constant signing algorithm in an exact real-RAM model; this makes the conditional cover construction algorithmic for explicit matrices but does not yield an efficient P-versus-NP argument at the project's dimensions. Section 13 also checks two public `P=NP` claims and identifies a direct proof-step failure in one and a missing membership-decision direction in the other's Lean chain. Section 15 records a gap in the pedigree paper's stated rigid-set size proof. Section 16 gives an independent flow-support argument yielding $|R_k|=O(k^5)$, conditional on the construction rules, and corrects a proposed extension-complexity objection: the paper's LP is built from each input point and is not shown to be a fixed extended formulation. Section 17 adds a source-level audit of the public Lean formalization: its P=NP chain assumes a custom polynomial-membership axiom, and its MCF type fails an explicit n=6 fractional member and omits shared capacity coupling. Sections 21.4-21.7 add a joint anchor-support closure, a firing-rule circuit compilation of size $O(Nm+m^3)$ from an $m$-pair cover, an exact two-pair theorem at the boundary of a sparse co-singleton shell, and a proof-DAG argument giving a near-linear all-semi-filter cover lower bound for Gap-MCSP. Section 23 applies Reed-Muller minimum distance to rule out low-degree polynomial-feature span filters as a source of cover lower bounds. Sections 24-25 strengthen that negative result: affine images of disjoint information sets give one disjoint covering pair for binary and q-ary Reed-Muller feature spans below half degree, under the exact orbit-avoidance condition $2|A|D<|\Omega|$. Finite checks support the closure computations, sparse toy theorem, binary and q-ary feature-span constructions, and the small blocking-set cases in Section 26. That section gives only an endpoint-size condition, not a pair-count lower bound. Section 27 audits a finite-combinatorics/ZFC independence proposal: its key independence claim is conjectural, and its stated P=NP implication needs an input-length bound and a clear multiset convention in the standard binary model. The analytic Gap-MCSP bound remains linear and below the magnification threshold; none of these results proves either outcome of P versus NP.

Section 22 audits a separate affine-plus-AND branch-collapse line from the accessible project mirror. It gives an exact $2^r\operatorname{poly}(N)$ SAT algorithm from a supplied control set of projected affine rank $r$, and a direct reduction showing that minimum-rank control selection is NP-hard in the classical sense. This turn identifies that selection problem exactly as Rank Vertex Cover over a binary linear matroid; the known rank-parameter FPT branching algorithm finds optimal controls in $2^{O(r)}\operatorname{poly}(N)$ time. Combined with affine-slice enumeration, this gives an end-to-end SAT algorithm in $2^{O(r^*)}\operatorname{poly}(N)$, where $r^*$ is the minimum projected control rank. Exhaustive and randomized checks support the implementation, but $r^*$ can be large, so this is not a general P-versus-NP result.

## 1. Executive verdict

The project has not produced seven unrelated approaches. Almost every line of work is an instance of one common problem:

> Given a succinctly represented map \(G\) and a target \(y\), represent, decide, find, avoid, or prove the emptiness of the fiber
> \[
> G^{-1}(y)=\{x:G(x)=y\}.
> \]

This **fiber/avoidance normal form** is the most useful missing link I found.

It unifies the project as follows.

| Project line | Succinct map | Fiber question |
|---|---|---|
| SAT | Boolean circuit \(C:\{0,1\}^n\to\{0,1\}\) | Is \(C^{-1}(1)\) nonempty? |
| Backward vectors/tensors | Gate tensor network for \(C\) | Can the pinned-output fiber be manipulated semantically? |
| MCSP | Truth-table generator \(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}\): circuit descriptions \(\to\) truth tables | Is \(b\in\operatorname{Range}(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s})\)? |
| \(K^t\), Kt, MKtP | Bounded universal evaluator: programs \(\to\) strings | Does \(b\) have a short/fast preimage program? |
| Proper circuit learning | Partial truth-table generator | Is there a small circuit in the sample-consistency fiber? |
| Residual SAT atlas | Restricted map \(C\!\upharpoonright_\alpha\) | Does the target remain in the restricted range? |
| Proof search | CNF \(\tau_y(G)\) encoding \(G(x)=y\) | If the fiber is empty, how hard is that to prove? |
| Range Avoidance/Berry diagonalization | Same \(G\) | Find \(y\notin\operatorname{Range}(G)\) |
| Fusion/cover complexity | Range-membership predicate | How large a circuit is needed to decide empty versus nonempty fibers? |

Three rigorous conclusions came out of this audit.

### Conclusion A ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â the exact factored preimage is always polynomial-size

For a size-\(s\) Boolean circuit, the backward preimage of output \(1\) always has an exact tensor/factor-network representation of size \(O(s)\), provided internal wire variables are retained. This answers the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s highlighted question affirmatively if ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œkept in factored formÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â refers only to representation size.

The catch is exact and unavoidable: deciding whether this compact factor network represents the zero vector is SAT-complete, and contracting it to obtain its norm or number of supported inputs is #P-complete. Polynomial representation size is therefore not polynomial semantic access.

### Conclusion B ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â there is a clean vector-direction construction with a constant gap

Every 3-CNF \(F\) has a polynomial-dimensional coefficient vector \(c_F\) and a degree-three feature vector \(\phi(p)\), \(p\in[0,1]^n\), such that
\[
E_F(p)=c_F\cdot\phi(p)
\]
is the expected number of violated clauses under independent randomized rounding with marginals \(p\). Consequently:

* if \(F\) is satisfiable, some \(p\) has \(E_F(p)=0\);
* if \(F\) is unsatisfiable, every \(p\) has \(E_F(p)\ge 1\);
* any \(p\) with \(E_F(p)<1\) can be rounded in polynomial time to a satisfying assignment.

Thus the normalized output vector
\[
v_F(p)=\frac{(E_F(p),1)}{\sqrt{1+E_F(p)^2}}
\]
has the desired direction \((0,1)\) exactly in the satisfiable case, while every unsatisfiable instance stays at least \(\pi/4\) away. This removes ambiguity about precision and gives a particularly faithful realization of the proposed direction idea.

It does not give a SAT algorithm: reversing the dot product means intersecting a hyperplane with the Boolean degree-three Veronese set \(\phi(\{0,1\}^n)\), and that intersection problem is precisely SAT.

### Conclusion C ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â fusion cover gives a conditional hardness-magnification route

A 2025 paper of Cavalar and Oliveira shows that the fusion method is captured by a two-dimensional semi-filter cover measure \(\rho\), and that \(\rho\) is exactly cyclic intersection complexity. This is strikingly close to the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s Atlas Fusion Complexity \(\Phi\), including the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s insistence on cycles, rewrites, and unrestricted sharing.

Combining that result with the OliveiraÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“PichÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Santhanam hardness-magnification theorem gives the following exact conditional route:

> If one proves a slightly superlinear fusion-cover lower bound for the promise version of Gap-MCSP at the magnification parameters, then \(NP\not\subseteq P/\mathrm{poly}\), and hence \(P\ne NP\).

The proposed unrestricted row/column graph proxy for this bound collapses to a single rectangle on the restricted promise domain (Proposition 12.1), so it cannot establish the premise. The direct bit-literal promise-cover lower bound remains a valid conditional target. The canonical filters and several natural measure-defined substitutes are also covered by short lists (Propositions 12.3ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“12.7). A successful construction must therefore use a different noncanonical semi-filter family and control intersection closure. This remains an open gap in the project, not a completed proof.

---

## 2. The master object: a pinned fiber

Let \(G:\{0,1\}^a\to\{0,1\}^b\) be a Boolean circuit and fix \(y\in\{0,1\}^b\). There are six distinct tasks that must not be conflated.

1. **Represent:** Store the relation \(G(x)=y\) succinctly.
2. **Decide:** Determine whether \(G^{-1}(y)\) is empty.
3. **Search:** Find an \(x\in G^{-1}(y)\) when one exists.
4. **Count:** Compute \(|G^{-1}(y)|\).
5. **Avoid:** Find a \(y\) for which \(G^{-1}(y)=\varnothing\).
6. **Prove:** Produce a short formal certificate that \(G^{-1}(y)=\varnothing\).

The project repeatedly solved Task 1 and then implicitly treated Tasks 2ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“6 as if they came for free. They do not.

### 2.1 SAT, MCSP, and MKtP are the same geometric question at different levels

For SAT, \(G=C\) and \(y=1\). For MCSP, define the truth-table generator
\[
\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}:\{0,1\}^{d(n,s)}\longrightarrow\{0,1\}^{N},\qquad N=2^n,
\]
which interprets its input as a description of an \(n\)-input circuit of size at most \(s\) and outputs that circuitÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s truth table. Then
\[
b\in\operatorname{Range}(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s})
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{CC}(b)\le s.
\]

For time-bounded Kolmogorov complexity, replace \(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}\) by a bounded universal evaluator that maps a program (and, for probabilistic variants, random bits) to its output string. Membership in its range is the short-program question.

The levels therefore line up exactly:
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{SAT}&:\quad 1\in\operatorname{Range}(C),\\
\mathrm{MCSP}&:\quad b\in\operatorname{Range}(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}),\\
\mathrm{MKtP}&:\quad b\in\operatorname{Range}(U_{s,t}).
\end{aligned}
\]

### 2.2 Range Avoidance is the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s Berry diagonal in its mature form

For the truth-table generator,
\[
b\notin\operatorname{Range}(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s})
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{CC}(b)>s.
\]
So ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œconstruct a truth table not produced by any small circuitÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â is exactly a Range-Avoidance problem. This identifies the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s earlier Berry-style diagonalization with a major current circuit-lower-bound program, rather than merely a failed counting trick.

The old obstructionÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Âconstructing the whole \(2^n\)-bit table does not give efficient local access to its bitsÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Âis also exactly the modern obstruction. A different random missing string on each execution does not define one hard language. One needs a consistent or single-valued construction whose bit graph lies in a sufficiently low complexity class. Recent work obtains strong lower bounds for larger exponential-time classes using this idea; bringing the output all the way down to NP would already be a breakthrough of the desired kind.

### 2.3 Proof complexity uses the same pinned fiber

Let \(\tau_y(G)\) be the standard CNF with input variables and internal history variables expressing \(G(x)=y\). Then:

* if \(y\in\operatorname{Range}(G)\), \(\tau_y(G)\) is satisfiable and a preimage is a witness;
* if \(y\notin\operatorname{Range}(G)\), \(\tau_y(G)\) is unsatisfiable and a refutation proves avoidance.

The 2026 literature on proof-complexity generators studies exactly these formulas. A generator is hard for a proof system when statements of the form \(y\notin\operatorname{Range}(G)\) have no short proofs. Thus the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s backward vector, its proof-learning experiments, and its hard-truth-table search meet on precisely the same CNF.

## 3. Exact answer to the backward-preimage factorization question

### Theorem 1 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â linear-size exact tensor representation

Let \(C\) be a Boolean circuit with \(n\) input wires, \(s\) internal gates of fan-in at most two, and one output. There is an exact tensor network of size \(O(n+s)\), with every index of dimension two and every tensor of constant arity, whose open input tensor is the vector
\[
\psi_C\in\{0,1\}^{2^n},\qquad \psi_C[x]=C(x).
\]

#### Proof

Give every wire a Boolean index. For a binary gate \(g\), define the constant-size relation tensor
\[
T_g(a,b,c)=
\begin{cases}
1,&c=g(a,b),\\
0,&\text{otherwise}.
\end{cases}
\]
Use the analogous rank-two tensor for a unary gate and equality tensors for fanout. Let \(y_1,\ldots,y_s\) denote the internal wire values, and pin the output index to \(1\). The resulting open tensor is
\[
\psi_C(x)
=
\sum_{y\in\{0,1\}^s}
\mathbf 1[y_{\mathrm{out}}=1]
\prod_{g\in C}T_g(y_{\mathrm{in}_1(g)},y_{\mathrm{in}_2(g)},y_g).
\]
For each fixed \(x\), determinism of the circuit gives exactly one consistent assignment to the internal wires. The product is one for that assignment and zero for every other assignment. Hence \(\psi_C(x)=C(x)\). There is one constant-size tensor per gate or fanout relation, so the description has size \(O(n+s)\). ÃƒÂ¢Ã‹â€ Ã…Â½

This is the literal backward preimage vector in a polynomial-size exact factored form. No restricted OBDD, DNNF, bounded-rank, or bounded-treewidth assumption is being imposed.

### Corollary 1 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â nonzero testing is SAT and contraction is #SAT

Because \(\psi_C\) is Boolean-valued,
\[
C\text{ is satisfiable}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\psi_C\ne 0,
\]
and
\[
\|\psi_C\|_2^2
=\sum_x \psi_C(x)^2
=\#\{x:C(x)=1\}.
\]

Therefore:

* deciding whether such a factored vector is nonzero is NP-complete, while deciding that it is identically zero is coNP-complete;
* exact norm computation is #P-complete;
* the factor description can remain linear even when every known exact contraction ordering is exponential.

The projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s highlighted question has a split answer:

* **Polynomial-size exact factorization:** yes, unconditionally.
* **Polynomial-time useful manipulation of that factorization:** this is exactly the unresolved computational problem.

### Theorem 2 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â a semantic-access compiler is equivalent to \(P=NP\)

Consider any uniform representation scheme with algorithms satisfying all of the following.

1. \(\mathrm{Compile}(C)\) runs in polynomial time and outputs a polynomial-size exact representation of \(C^{-1}(1)\).
2. \(\mathrm{Empty}(R)\) decides in polynomial time whether the represented set is empty.
3. Optionally, \(\mathrm{Restrict}(R,i,b)\) conditions a represented set on \(x_i=b\) in polynomial time.

Then the first two operations imply \(P=NP\). With restriction as well, a satisfying assignment can be recovered by the standard bit-by-bit self-reduction. Conversely, if \(P=NP\), the representation can simply store \(C\); emptiness is decided by the polynomial SAT algorithm and restrictions are circuit substitutions.

Thus, for unrestricted exact representations, the hoped-for package ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œcompact + exact + efficient nonemptinessÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â is not merely reminiscent of SAT. Under the normal uniform Turing-machine model, its existence is equivalent to \(P=NP\).

### 3.1 Why gate reversal branches

An AND gate has
\[
\mathrm{AND}^{-1}(1)=\{(1,1)\},\qquad
\mathrm{AND}^{-1}(0)=\{(0,0),(0,1),(1,0)\}.
\]
The inverse is a relation, not a function. OR has the dual behavior. Reversing a circuit therefore produces intersections from shared wires and unions from alternative local preimages. Retaining those alternatives as factors is compact; distributing the unions is where exponential expansion can appear.

Reconvergent fanout is the essential coupling. If two backward branches share an earlier wire, they cannot select its value independently. Equality tensors preserve this consistency, but they also create loops and high contraction width.

### 3.2 The reversible-computation trilemma

Making every gate reversible does not remove the issue. A reversible lift has the form
\[
U_C:(x,0^a)\longmapsto(x,\mathrm{garbage}(x),C(x)).
\]
The full map is bijective, so a fully specified output can be inverted. But specifying only the final acceptance bit leaves the other output coordinates unknown. Selecting compatible garbage is exactly selecting a satisfying \(x\).

This gives a simple trilemma.

1. **Keep the map invertible:** the output must retain enough information to distinguish inputs, so the desired target is a large subspace/fiber rather than one known point.
2. **Collapse every satisfying input to one target:** the map becomes many-to-one, so its inverse is set-valued.
3. **Aggregate all preimages into amplitudes, norms, or directions:** deciding zero versus nonzero becomes SAT, and exact counting generally becomes #SAT.

### 3.3 Infinite precision is not a loophole

A real coordinate can formally encode exponentially many bits. A unit-cost real-RAM algorithm or a construction using noncomputable constants could therefore conceal the entire SAT truth table in one direction. That is not a polynomial-time Turing computation. Exact rational/algebraic coordinates must include their bit complexity; exponentially precise angles or exponentially long numerators are exponential representations in disguise.

---

## 4. A concrete vector-direction construction for 3-SAT

The following construction is both simpler and stronger than the earlier matrix proposals because it has polynomial dimension and a constant soundness gap.

Let
\[
F=\bigwedge_{j=1}^{m} C_j
\]
be a CNF with clauses of width at most three, after the harmless preprocessing that removes tautological clauses and duplicate literal occurrences. Thus the literals within a clause depend on distinct variables. For \(p\in[0,1]^n\), define the false-probability of a literal by
\[
q_{x_i}(p)=1-p_i,
\qquad
q_{\neg x_i}(p)=p_i.
\]
Define
\[
E_F(p)=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\prod_{\ell\in C_j}q_{\ell}(p).
\]

### Theorem 3 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â constant-gap direction theorem

1. If \(F\) is satisfiable, then \(\min_{p\in[0,1]^n}E_F(p)=0\).
2. If \(F\) is unsatisfiable, then \(E_F(p)\ge 1\) for every \(p\in[0,1]^n\).
3. Given any \(p\) with \(E_F(p)<1\), a satisfying Boolean assignment can be found in polynomial time.

#### Proof

Sample independent Boolean variables \(X_i\sim\mathrm{Bernoulli}(p_i)\). For each clause \(C_j\),
\[
\Pr[C_j(X)\text{ is false}]
=\prod_{\ell\in C_j}q_{\ell}(p).
\]
Hence
\[
E_F(p)=\mathbb E[\text{number of clauses violated by }X].
\]

If \(F\) is satisfiable, take \(p\) to be a satisfying Boolean vertex, giving expectation zero. If \(F\) is unsatisfiable, every Boolean assignment violates at least one clause, so the random variable inside the expectation is always at least one and \(E_F(p)\ge1\).

For the rounding claim, fix variables one at a time. The current expectation is a convex combination of the two conditional expectations obtained by setting the next variable to zero or one. Choose the setting with no larger conditional expectation. At the end, the number of violated clauses is an integer strictly below one, hence zero. ÃƒÂ¢Ã‹â€ Ã…Â½

### 4.1 One large coefficient vector

Expand every degree-three product. Let
\[
\phi(p)=\bigl(1,\ p_i,\ p_ip_j,\ p_ip_jp_k\bigr)_{i,j,k}
\]
contain all square-free monomials of degree at most three. Its dimension is
\[
1+n+\binom n2+\binom n3=O(n^3).
\]
There is an integer coefficient vector \(c_F\) with polynomial bit complexity such that
\[
E_F(p)=c_F\cdot\phi(p).
\]
Define the output direction
\[
v_F(p)=\frac{(c_F\cdot\phi(p),1)}{\sqrt{1+(c_F\cdot\phi(p))^2}}.
\]
Then
\[
F\in\mathrm{SAT}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\exists p\in[0,1]^n:\ v_F(p)=(0,1).
\]
For an unsatisfiable formula, every attainable direction is at angular distance at least \(\arctan(1)=\pi/4\) from \((0,1)\).

This is a direct realization of ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œthe circuit is one vector \(c\), the input is a vector, and output 1 is a desired direction.ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

### 4.2 Why vector subtraction does not recover the input

Setting the first coordinate to zero gives the hyperplane equation
\[
c_F\cdot z=0.
\]
But a valid feature vector must satisfy
\[
z=\phi(p),\qquad p\in\{0,1\}^n
\]
or, in the relaxed formulation, the nonconvex consistency relations among all degree-two and degree-three monomials. Intersecting the hyperplane with this Boolean Veronese image is exactly SAT.

If the coordinates of \(z\) are treated as independent, the problem becomes a weak linear relaxation and loses soundness. If all rank-one/moment consistency constraints are retained, the original combinatorial difficulty returns.

Taking the convex hull does not create a hidden shortcut. Linear optimization over
\[
\operatorname{conv}\bigl(\phi(\{0,1\}^n)\bigr)
\]
already contains Max-3SAT. A uniformly constructible polynomial-size exact linear lift with efficient separation would therefore put SAT in P. LP/SDP or sum-of-squares relaxations can expose useful structure at low levels, but arbitrary formulas can require the hierarchy to retain exponentially much global consistency.

The direction therefore has a constant gap; precision is not the missing ingredient. The missing operation is global optimization over a nonconvex, exponentially rich feasible set.

### 4.3 A useful conservation principle

Any proposed general vector construction appears forced to place the hardness in at least one of four locations:

* exponential dimension;
* exponential bit precision;
* a nonconvex or discrete feasible set;
* a semantic operation such as zero testing, projection, contraction, or optimization that is itself NP/#P-hard.

Our construction uses polynomial dimension, polynomial precision, and a constant gap, so it exposes the hardness cleanly in the third and fourth locations.

### 4.4 Exotic variants stress-tested

**Quantum amplitudes.** A uniform superposition can evaluate all assignments coherently, but the accepting weight is \(\#\mathrm{SAT}/2^n\). With one witness this is exponentially small; amplitude amplification gives GroverÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s quadratic improvement, not a polynomial algorithm. Postselection can magnify tiny branches only by changing the computational model to a much stronger class.

**Complex interference.** Allowing signed or complex amplitudes introduces cancellation, so a zero aggregate need not mean an empty fiber. Preventing cancellation with nonnegative weights returns to support or counting. Designing a cancellation pattern that is sound for every formula is itself a global identity/zero-testing problem.

**Fourier, Clifford, exterior, or geometric algebra.** These bases can make XOR and convolution transparent. The full algebra normally has one basis blade/character per subset of variables, hence dimension \(2^n\). Succinct circuits can describe elements of that algebra, but coefficient extraction or Boolean-support testing retains the semantic hardness.

**Tropical/min-plus algebra.** Changing the contraction semiring from \((+,\times)\) to \((\min,+)\) turns contraction into minimum violated clauses or weighted Max-SAT. This is excellent on bounded-treewidth networks and still NP-hard in general.

**Convex or semidefinite lifting.** A polynomial-size exact convex formulation with an efficient separation oracle and a constant promise gap would yield a polynomial SAT algorithm. Known LP, SDP, and sum-of-squares hierarchies are useful restricted relaxations; making them exact in general requires retaining global moment information.

**Homotopy and gradient flow.** Continuously deforming an easy formula into the target can create and destroy solution components, and following one branch can become ill-conditioned. The constant-gap objective removes one numerical excuse but still has combinatorial local minima and an NP-hard global minimum.

**Topological invariants.** The accepting set of a succinct circuit can encode an arbitrary succinct subset of the Boolean cube. Computing nonemptiness, connected components, or related invariants of succinctly represented complexes can therefore inherit the same hardness. Topology helps only after proving special structure of the represented family.

**Random isolation.** Hashing can reduce SAT to a zero-or-one-solution promise with randomized reductions, but it does not turn the isolated witness into a known vector coordinate. Derandomizing isolation is itself linked to circuit lower bounds.

**Noncomputable constants or exact analog devices.** A fixed real direction can encode an undecidable or NP-complete language in its binary expansion. Such advice is not a uniform finite algorithm and is excluded by ordinary definitions of P and NP.

---

## 5. The project audit, line by line

### 5.1 Matrix, vector, and tensor representations

**What is valid**

* Gate tensors give an exact \(O(s)\)-size representation of the accepting preimage.
* The degree-three coefficient-vector construction gives a constant-margin direction formulation.
* Basis changes can reveal tractable algebraic structure, especially parity/affine structure.

**What does not follow**

* Compactness does not imply an efficient emptiness or norm query.
* A Boolean gate is not invertible as a map from its input bits to one output bit.
* A change of basis conjugates the same computation; it helps only if the transformed signatures land in a tractable family.

**Closest established frameworks**

* Tensor networks and Holant/#CSP.
* ValiantÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s holographic algorithms and matchgate transformations.
* Knowledge compilation, which explicitly separates succinctness from supported queries and transformations.
* Boolean polynomial systems and algebraic proof systems.

The Holant dichotomy literature is particularly relevant. Holographic basis transformations solve remarkable special classes, but broad dichotomy theorems classify the remaining signature families as #P-hard. This is essentially the mature version of the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œrotate the directions until the constraints simplifyÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â idea.

### 5.2 Residual SAT atlas and fusion quantities

The residual viability atlas
\[
V(F,\alpha)=1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad F\!\upharpoonright_\alpha\text{ is satisfiable}
\]
is a useful conceptual object. But its root entry is SAT itself, and its full table has exponentially many partial assignments.

When generator sets are literals and allowed operations are union/intersection, the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s unrestricted fusion complexity is essentially discrete/De Morgan circuit complexity. The 2025 CavalarÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Oliveira paper establishes the closest formal match found in this audit:

* discrete complexity \(D(A\mid\mathcal B)\) is the minimum number of unions/intersections constructing a target set;
* for Boolean literals, this is De Morgan circuit complexity;
* cover complexity satisfies
  \[
  \rho(A,\mathcal B)\le D_\cap(A\mid\mathcal B)\le \rho(A,\mathcal B)^2;
  \]
* exactly,
  \[
  \rho(A,\mathcal B)=D^{\mathrm{cyc}}_\cap(A\mid\mathcal B),
  \]
  the cyclic intersection complexity.

Thus cycles, feedback-style propagation, arbitrary sharing, and unrestricted set fusions do not escape circuit complexity. They characterize it, up to the stated polynomial relationship.

The decomposition
\[
\phi=E+\Lambda+\sigma
\]
is coherent accounting, but accounting identities do not themselves lower-bound the minimized quantity \(\Phi\). The difficult step remains an explicit invariant that every allowed fusion changes by little while the target has a large value.

### 5.3 Orbit, canonical programs, Kt, and logical depth

**What survives**

* Kt-like measures correctly charge both description length and running time.
* A short, fast program can be viewed as a preimage under a bounded universal evaluator.
* Meta-complexity and conditional Kolmogorov complexity have deep, current connections to worst-case and average-case NP complexity.

**What fails as a direct proof**

* A short description of a whole truth table does not imply a small circuit for its individual bits.
* A canonical shortest or fastest program need not be efficiently discoverable.
* Generic ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œfastest algorithmÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â reasoning is unsafe: Blum speedup shows that computable functions can lack an optimal algorithm under broad measures.
* Levin universal search is optimal up to a program-dependent constant, but that constant may be exponential in the description length and therefore does not yield polynomial SAT.

Recent exact search-to-decision work for \(K^t\) confirms that the extraction issue is a substantial research problem, not a routine binary search. The strongest results are average-case, randomized, conditional on circuit hardness, or subexponential in the worst case.

### 5.4 Gap-MINLT and predictor extraction

The intended proof pattern was sound:

1. a SAT witness gives a short fast perfect predictor;
2. an UNSAT instance should make every allowed predictor nearly random;
3. an efficient approximation algorithm would collapse an NP class into randomized polynomial time.

The missing implication was always
\[
\text{noticeably good predictor}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad\text{satisfying assignment}.
\]
Naive labels fail because a constant predictor can correlate with them. Hadamard-style labels are decodable, but computing the labels from the formula without the witness recreates the original problem.

The conditional pKt literature shows that this is part of a mature meta-complexity frontier. Conditional pKt is NP-hard in a sublinear-access regime; extending hardness to polynomial-time regimes would have major consequences for ImpagliazzoÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s worlds. The project should reuse those coding constructions rather than inventing an unconstrained predictor label from scratch.

The vector polynomial in Section 4 supplies a clean extraction lemma of the same flavor: \(E_F(p)<1\) implies a witness by conditional expectations. It does not supply an efficient method for finding such a \(p\).

### 5.5 Proper learning of circuits

The simple conditional theorem is correct:

> If \(P=NP\), polynomial-size circuits are efficiently properly PAC learnable from labeled examples, given a polynomial size bound.

Under \(P=NP\), one can decide and self-reduce the NP search problem ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œfind a circuit of size at most \(s\) consistent with this sample.ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â Standard Occam/sample-complexity reasoning gives generalization. Therefore, an unconditional impossibility theorem for efficient proper learning of polynomial-size circuits would imply \(P\ne NP\).

But proving such an impossibility is not easier by default. Learning, natural properties, pseudorandom functions, and gap versions of MCSP are linked by known equivalences. This is not an independent escape hatch; it is another face of meta-complexity and circuit lower bounds.

### 5.6 MCSP and the Berry diagonal

The earlier counting argument correctly says almost all truth tables require large circuits. It did not produce an explicit, locally accessible hard function. The description ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œthe first truth table outside the small-circuit rangeÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â is short, but computing it can require enumerating an exponential range, and a short global generation algorithm does not automatically yield a small circuit for each indexed bit.

The right modern identification is:

* the truth-table generatorÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s range is the set of small-circuit truth tables;
* MCSP is range membership;
* a hard truth table is a missing output;
* constructing one consistently is Range Avoidance;
* proving it hard is the pinned-fiber tautology \(\tau_b(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s})\).

Total MCSP is still not known NP-hard under ordinary deterministic many-one reductions. Several partial, restricted, multi-output, and randomized-reduction variants are hard, but those results cannot be silently transferred to total general-circuit MCSP.

### 5.7 Neural proof search and interpretability

The experiments produced real empirical results:

* successful reconstruction on several synthetic proof families;
* a meaningful improvement over greedy search on the deceptive DAG benchmark;
* clear evidence that intermediate layers encode more UNSAT-core information than the input layer;
* a sharp layer-two patch in one controlled representation experiment;
* later direct-policy interventions showing mostly distributed, not single-head, causal effects.

They do not establish proof-length lower bounds, polynomial automatizability, or a general SAT algorithm. Resolution lower bounds apply to resolution, not all SAT algorithms. Extended Resolution and Extended Frege are much stronger, and superpolynomial lower bounds for them remain major open problems.

The best theoretical use of the neural work is as a conjecture generator for **theory discovery**:

* detect affine/XOR structure and invoke Gaussian elimination;
* identify Horn, dual-Horn, 2-SAT, or other tractable CSP structure;
* propose extension variables or lemmas;
* discover semi-filter or avoidance witnesses on small instances.

The most relevant next benchmark is not another small ordinary pigeonhole formula. It is \(\tau_b(G)\) for truth-table generators, low-degree XORÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“AND generators, and Range-Avoidance/proof-complexity-generator instances. Those formulas sit directly on the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s newly identified spine.

### 5.8 Pigeonhole proofs and dual weak pigeonhole

The projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s strong performance on small pigeonhole examples has a previously missed conceptual connection. Range Avoidance is the computational dual weak pigeonhole principle: a map from a smaller domain to a larger codomain must miss an output.

Ordinary PHP refutations and dual weak PHP/range-avoidance principles behave very differently in strong proof systems and bounded arithmetic. Efficient small-instance proof reconstruction therefore does not transfer, but the shared pigeonhole geometry suggests a principled curriculum: ordinary PHP \(\to\) implicit PHP \(\to\) range-avoidance fibers \(\to\) proof-complexity generators.

### 5.9 Compression and kernels

An exact factor graph is not automatically an instance compression. It may retain all variables and merely change syntax. Known kernelization and OR-compression lower bounds rule out certain strong compression procedures unless standard collapses occur, but they are conditional and do not prove \(P\ne NP\).

The relevant distinction is:

* **syntactic compression:** a small object still requiring a hard query;
* **semantic compression:** a small object in a language supporting the needed query efficiently.

The project repeatedly achieved the first and sought the second.

### 5.10 Concept invention, extensions, and XOR

Extension variables can drastically shorten proofs and lower structural width. This is why lower bounds confined to resolution, bounded width, OBDDs, or one fixed tensor ordering are not enough for the userÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s unrestricted target.

But allowing arbitrary extensions merely moves the question to discovering useful extensions. The 2026 work on simple circuit extensions is directly relevant: XORÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s optimal circuits are structured enough that its simple-extension problem is in P, while multiplexer-like functions are proposed as more promising candidates for hardness. This supports the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s empirical observation that affine/Tseitin structure requires a qualitatively different solver, not more of the same resolution-style policy.

---

## 6. The avoidance spine

The deepest cross-link in the audit is the following chain.

\[
\boxed{\text{compact pinned fiber}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{range membership / emptiness}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{avoidance witness}}
\longrightarrow
\boxed{\text{circuit lower bound or hard tautology}}.
\]

### 6.1 Truth-table avoidance

For \(G=\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}\), a missing output is an explicit truth table requiring circuits larger than \(s\). A single-valued construction of such outputs in a low uniform class defines a hard language in that class.

This explains simultaneously:

* why random sampling is easy but insufficientÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Âdifferent runs define different truth tables;
* why canonicalization mattersÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Âthe output must be consistent;
* why lexicographically first is not a free solutionÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Âits bits can be much harder to compute;
* why the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s local-decoding gap is central, not cosmetic.

### 6.2 Fusion lower bounds are generalized avoidance

In the fusion framework, each proposed intersection pair covers a collection of semi-filters. To prove \(\rho(A,\mathcal B)>r\), one must show:
\[
\text{for every list of }r\text{ proposed pairs, there exists a semi-filter not covered by any pair.}
\]
That is a generalized missing-object problem. The sought semi-filter is an avoidance witness.

This is where the Berry, fusion, and Range-Avoidance lines genuinely meet. Counting proves that an uncovered object exists for random targets; an explicit lower bound requires enough structure to locate or certify one against every proposed cover.

### 6.3 Vector and XOR avoidance

The newest algebrization-barrier work studies XOR-Missing-String. This is relevant because several project proposals hoped that Fourier, parity, or vector-space coordinates would make avoidance and inversion simpler. The new results do not rule out the project, but they warn that purely algebraic/XOR-based avoidance arguments can remain trapped behind algebrization barriers.

### 6.4 Proof-complexity generators

For \(y\notin\operatorname{Range}(G)\), the formula \(\tau_y(G)\) asserts an empty fiber. Proof-complexity generators are maps for which these avoidance tautologies remain hard for a chosen proof system. Therefore:

* tensor preimage emptiness supplies the formula;
* range avoidance supplies the missing target;
* proof complexity measures the cost of certifying the target is missing;
* neural proof search can probe small instances of the same family.

This is the best bridge from the empirical proof work to the theoretical lower-bound program.

---

## 7. The strongest combined theorem: promise fusion magnification

This section states the most consequential exact implication found in the audit. The formulation appears to be new to this project; I did not find this exact promise-cover corollary stated in the surveyed sources. It is, however, a direct combination of published theorems, not a new proof of their premises.

### 7.1 Definitions

Let \(N=2^n\) be the truth-table input length. Let \(c\ge1\) be the universal constant in the OliveiraÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“PichÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Santhanam theorem. For a small constant \(\beta>0\), define
\[
s_1(n,\beta)=\frac{2^{\beta n}}{cn},
\qquad
s_2(n,\beta)=2^{\beta n}.
\]
Let
\[
Y_{n,\beta}=\{z\in\{0,1\}^{N}:\operatorname{CC}(z)\le s_1(n,\beta)\},
\]
and
\[
Z_{n,\beta}=\{z\in\{0,1\}^{N}:\operatorname{CC}(z)\ge s_2(n,\beta)\}.
\]
The promise ground set is
\[
\Gamma_{n,\beta}=Y_{n,\beta}\cup Z_{n,\beta}.
\]
For each input position \(i\in[N]\) and bit \(b\in\{0,1\}\), let
\[
B_{i,b}=\{z\in\Gamma_{n,\beta}:z_i=b\}.
\]
Let \(\mathcal B_{N}^{\Gamma}=\{B_{i,b}\}\). Define the **promise fusion-cover complexity**
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{GapMCSP}}(n,\beta)
=
\rho\bigl(Y_{n,\beta},\mathcal B_N^{\Gamma}\bigr).
\]

Working on the restricted ground set is essential. Proving a lower bound for one arbitrary totalization of the promise would not exclude a circuit that behaves differently in the gap. On \(\Gamma\), a correct promise separator computes exactly the target set \(Y\).

### Theorem 4 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Promise Fusion Magnification Lemma

Suppose there exists \(\varepsilon>0\) such that, for every sufficiently small \(\beta>0\), the family above satisfies
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{GapMCSP}}(n,\beta)>N^{1+\varepsilon}
\]
with the asymptotic strength required for a nonuniform circuit lower bound. Then
\[
NP\not\subseteq P/\mathrm{poly},
\]
and consequently \(P\ne NP\).

#### Proof

On the promise ground set, every De Morgan circuit separating \(Y_{n,\beta}\) from \(Z_{n,\beta}\) corresponds gate-for-gate to a construction of \(Y_{n,\beta}\) from the literal generator sets using union and intersection; push negations to the inputs. Hence its circuit size is at least the discrete complexity, and in particular at least the intersection complexity:
\[
\operatorname{CircuitSize}(\mathrm{GapMCSP})
\ge D_\cap(Y_{n,\beta}\mid\mathcal B_N^\Gamma).
\]
The CavalarÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Oliveira fusion inequality gives
\[
\rho(Y_{n,\beta},\mathcal B_N^\Gamma)
\le D_\cap(Y_{n,\beta}\mid\mathcal B_N^\Gamma).
\]
Therefore the assumed cover lower bound implies
\[
\mathrm{GapMCSP}[2^{\beta n}/(cn),2^{\beta n}]
\notin \mathrm{Circuit}[N^{1+\varepsilon}].
\]
The OliveiraÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“PichÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Santhanam hardness-magnification theorem then yields
\[
NP\not\subseteq \mathrm{Circuit}[\mathrm{poly}].
\]
Since \(P\subseteq P/\mathrm{poly}\), this implies \(P\ne NP\). ÃƒÂ¢Ã‹â€ Ã…Â½

### 7.2 A failed two-dimensional partial-graph proxy

Assume \(N\) is even and put \(K=2^{N/2}\). Split each \(N\)-bit string into two \(N/2\)-bit halves and identify it with a pair in \([K]\times[K]\). Transport the promise set \(\Gamma\) and target \(Y\) through this bijection. Restrict every row and column star to the promise domain.

The transference lemma for intersection complexity would make a lower bound for this graph cover sufficient, because every bit-literal preimage is a union of rows or columns and costs zero intersections. Proposition 12.1 falsifies this proxy: on the restricted promise domain, the positive set is one rectangle, so its graph intersection and cover complexities are at most one. This graph lower bound cannot hold in the stated Gap-MCSP regime. The direct bit-literal promise-cover problem remains distinct and open.

### 7.3 Why this is not merely a renamed proof

The theorem does three useful things.

1. It lowers the quantitative target from a superpolynomial SAT lower bound to a slightly superlinear lower bound in the meta-problemÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s input length.
2. It puts the target into a two-dimensional cover language where fractional-cover duality, entropy, spectral ideas, additive combinatorics, and computer search might be brought to bear.
3. It explicitly joins the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s fusion invariant to its Gap-MINLT/MCSP line.

But it does not make the missing bound easy. Fusion is complete enough to characterize cyclic circuit complexity; completeness is not a lower-bound technique.

### 7.4 The locality stress test

Hardness magnification can evade the usual natural-proofs heuristic because it exploits a special meta-computational predicate rather than a large property of random functions. However, known magnification proofs also provide small circuits augmented with arbitrary low-arity oracle gates. Many standard lower-bound methods continue to work even after those gates are added, so they cannot distinguish the target from its unconditional oracle upper bound.

Pich proved broad localizability theorems for approximation-method lower bounds. For unrestricted circuits, the strongest full-localizability statement remains open, and the fusion formulation is technically outside some of the cleanest hypotheses. Therefore this is a warning and a falsification test, not a theorem that the route is impossible.

A candidate proof must answer:

> Where does the argument use a global consistency property that fails in the presence of arbitrary local oracle gates?

If it has no answer, it is likely re-encountering the locality barrier.

### 7.5 A fractional-cover attack

The cover formulation suggests a concrete dual route. Let \(\mathcal F\) be the relevant semi-filters and let each pair \(q\) cover a subset \(S_q\subseteq\mathcal F\). If one constructs a probability distribution \(\mu\) on semi-filters such that
\[
\sup_q\mu(S_q)\le N^{-1-\varepsilon},
\]
then every cover needs at least \(N^{1+\varepsilon}\) pairs.

This is just the fractional set-cover lower bound, but it gives an explicit object to seek: a nonlocal distribution over Gap-MCSP semi-filters under which every single fusion pair has tiny influence.

The danger is that standard product, low-degree, random-restriction, or local distributions may localize. A successful \(\mu\) likely must encode cross-instance consistency about circuit descriptions or range membership, not merely local statistics of truth-table bits.

---

## 8. Proof attempts that fail, with the exact failure point

### Attempt 1 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œThe backward vector stays small, so inspect itÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** The factor network stays small, but zero testing is SAT and exact norm computation is #SAT.

### Attempt 2 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œSubtract or invert every gateÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** AND/OR are many-to-one. Their inverse is a union of alternatives, and fanout imposes global equality constraints between branches.

### Attempt 3 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œMake the circuit reversibleÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Pinning only the acceptance bit leaves unknown garbage. Choosing compatible garbage is the original witness search.

### Attempt 4 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œA direction gives a continuous optimization problemÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** The constant-gap formulation exists, but its feasible moment/feature set is nonconvex. Finding a point with objective below one is SAT-complete and already decodes to a witness.

### Attempt 5 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œUse polynomial identity testing on the circuit polynomialÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** SAT asks whether a polynomial is nonzero on the Boolean cube, not whether the formal polynomial is identically zero. Reducing modulo \(x_i^2-x_i\) and obtaining the unique multilinear coefficient vector can cause exponential blowup. Identity in the Boolean quotient ring is coNP-hard in this succinct setting.

### Attempt 6 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œRotate into the right basisÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** A basis change is an invertible equivalence. It is powerful on tractable Holant/CSP signatures, but an arbitrary 3-SAT instance is already built from one fixed NP-complete constraint language. A universal efficiently found tractable basis would itself be a SAT algorithm.

### Attempt 7 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œTensor rank/treewidth must growÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Rank and treewidth lower bounds apply to a selected representation family or contraction scheme. Arbitrary auxiliary variables, cyclic factors, basis changes, and mixed reasoning can evade any one such restriction. Conversely, allowing arbitrary cyclic factors preserves compactness but not tractable contraction.

### Attempt 8 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œThe canonical shortest/fastest program is the answerÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Existence does not imply efficient discovery. Time-bounded Kolmogorov search-to-decision is itself a major open/conditional frontier, and generic fastest-algorithm assumptions are invalidated by speedup phenomena.

### Attempt 9 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œThe first truth table missed by all small circuits is explicitÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** This is Range Avoidance. Computing a whole missing table in time polynomial in its length does not put its indexed-bit language in P or NP. Canonical/local output is the hard part.

### Attempt 10 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œA good predictor reveals a witnessÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Without an explicit decoding theorem, correlation can be achieved by trivial predictors. Witness-decodable codes require labels that are themselves hard to generate without the witness.

### Attempt 11 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œProper learning is impossible, therefore \(P\ne NP\)ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** The implication is valid only after proving the unconditional impossibility. That impossibility is linked to circuit lower bounds, natural properties, PRFs, and MCSP.

### Attempt 12 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œResolution blows up, so SAT is hardÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Resolution lower bounds constrain resolution. Other algorithms and stronger proof systems can bypass them. Extended Resolution has polynomial proofs of pigeonhole formulas that are exponentially hard for resolution.

### Attempt 13 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œExtended Resolution/Extended Frege is universal enoughÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** No known proof system is known to p-simulate every CookÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Reckhow proof system. A lower bound for one strong named system would be a major result but does not automatically prove that no other polynomially bounded proof system exists.

### Attempt 14 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œAn IPS lower bound proves \(P\ne NP\)ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** A superpolynomial IPS lower bound implies an algebraic circuit separation such as \(VP\ne VNP\); it is not known to imply \(P\ne NP\). IPS remains valuable because it ties proof lower bounds to arithmetic circuit lower bounds and PIT.

### Attempt 15 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œCompress SAT into the factor representationÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** The factor representation is a syntactic reformulation, not a smaller tractable instance. Known compression/kernel lower bounds apply only when the compressed object supports the necessary decision semantics.

### Attempt 16 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œUnique direction or unique witness removes the ambiguityÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** ValiantÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Vazirani shows SAT remains hard under randomized reductions even with a zero-or-one-witness promise. Multiplicity is not the central source of hardness. Canonical-witness verification can itself collapse complexity hierarchies.

### Attempt 17 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã…â€œThe fusion count is monotone along the chosen constructionÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã‚Â

**Failure:** Monotonicity in an anchored append-only process does not survive arbitrary preprocessing, extension variables, re-encoding, or choosing a globally different construction. The minimized invariant must quantify over all of them.

---

## 9. Barrier audit

### 9.1 Relativization

Any argument that treats subcircuits only as black-box input/output maps is at serious risk of relativizing. BakerÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“GillÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“Solovay constructed oracle worlds with both \(P=NP\) and \(P\ne NP\), so such an argument cannot settle the unrelativized problem by itself.

The factor-network construction is black-box compatible; the construction is valid, but any claimed lower bound extracted only from local inversion rules needs a clearly nonrelativizing step.

### 9.2 Natural proofs

An efficiently recognizable large property excluding all small circuits would conflict with strong pseudorandom generators. Direct truth-table rank, spectral, or random-function arguments often naturalize.

Gap-MCSP hardness magnification is attractive precisely because the predicate is special and meta-computational. Known results show some magnification hypotheses would themselves rule out natural properties. This weakens the natural-proofs objection but does not provide the needed lower bound.

### 9.3 Algebrization

Arithmetizing the circuit, using low-degree extensions, or applying Fourier/XOR transformations may still algebrize. The 2026 XOR-Missing-String work gives new oracle barriers specifically for algebraic avoidance techniques. Any vector proof should identify a step that cannot be simulated relative to an oracle and its low-degree extension.

### 9.4 Locality

This is the most specific danger to the fusion-magnification route. If a semi-filter distribution or gate approximation continues to work after inserting arbitrary low-fan-in oracle gates, it is probably too local to cross the magnification frontier.

### 9.5 Proof-system strength

Short proofs may exist after introducing the right concepts even when resolution explodes. Conversely, searching for the right concepts can be computationally hard. The 2025/2026 Proof Analysis Problem result shows that strong proof encodings can hide NP-hard information; proof analysis and witness extraction should not be assumed easy.

### 9.6 Total MCSP hardness

The NP-hardness of unrestricted total MCSP remains open. Project arguments may use partial MCSP, formula MCSP, multi-output MCSP, or randomized reductions only with their stated qualifications. Treating total MCSP as an ordinary NP-complete problem invalidates the proof chain.

---

## 10. Ranked research routes after the audit

### Route 1 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Promise fusion-cover lower bounds for Gap-MCSP

**Why it is first:** It is the only project synthesis found here with a short, exact theorem from one new combinatorial lower bound to \(P\ne NP\).

**Required lemma:** For every sufficiently small \(\beta\), prove
\[
\rho_{\mathrm{GapMCSP}}(n,\beta)>N^{1+\varepsilon}.
\]

**Most promising form:** Construct a fractional packing/distribution of nonlocal semi-filters and show every proposed fusion pair covers at most \(N^{-1-\varepsilon}\) mass. Proposition 12.3 rules out support only on canonical bit-literal filters; a successful distribution must use noncanonical filters.

**Mandatory falsification test:** Show exactly why the argument fails for circuits augmented with arbitrary local oracle gates.

**Main risk:** The cover measure is exactly cyclic intersection complexity, so the target remains an unrestricted circuit lower bound at the magnified scale.

### Route 2 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Single-valued Range Avoidance for truth-table generators

**Required lemma:** Construct a consistent missing output of \(\mathrm{TT}_{n,s}\) whose indexed-bit language lies in NP (or another sufficiently low class), for superpolynomial \(s(n)\).

**Consequence:** An explicit language in that class has superpolynomial circuit complexity; for NP this yields \(NP\not\subseteq P/\mathrm{poly}\).

**Connection to old work:** This is exactly the Berry/orbit/canonical-output proposal with the local-decoding obligation made explicit.

**Main risk:** Modern conditional hardness results suggest Range Avoidance can be hard even for nondeterministic search under cryptographic assumptions. Canonicalization is not an innocent postprocessing step.

### Route 3 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Fusion avoidance as a total-search problem

Given \(r\) proposed fusion pairs, output an uncovered semi-filter. Study the complexity of this generalized avoidance task directly.

**Potential bridge:** If this avoidance problem has a special single-valued or low-complexity solver for the Gap-MCSP target, it may furnish the lower-bound witness required by Route 1.

**Main risk:** Existence of an uncovered filter is equivalent to the desired lower bound; a generic solver may be as hard as the theorem.

### Route 4 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Conditional pKt in the polynomial-time regime

Reuse the coding, conditioning, and extraction machinery from current meta-complexity rather than inventing arbitrary predictor labels.

**Concrete target:** Understand whether the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s label/predictor construction can instantiate conditional pKt hardness beyond the known sublinear-access regime.

**Consequence:** Depending on the exact formulation, this can rule out intermediate Impagliazzo worlds or feed hardness magnification. It is not by itself a proof of \(P\ne NP\).

### Route 5 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Proof-complexity generators as the neural benchmark

Generate \(\tau_b(G)\) for:

* small truth-table generators;
* XOR\(\circ\)AND low-degree generators;
* simple-extension and multiplexer constructions;
* ordinary and dual weak pigeonhole progressions.

Use the model to propose extension variables, algebraic invariants, or semi-filter witnesses. Validate every proposal with a formal checker. This turns the neural work into a theorem-discovery instrument aimed at the actual bottleneck rather than a general SAT performance claim.

### Route 6 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Basis-adaptive SAT algorithms for restricted circuit classes

Combine Holant-style basis detection, Gaussian elimination, and learned theory selection to obtain nontrivial SAT algorithms for a robust restricted circuit class. Williams-style algorithm-to-lower-bound theorems can then produce nonuniform lower bounds for that class.

This is unlikely to settle \(P\) versus \(NP\) directly, but it is the most realistic path to an unconditional lower-bound advance from the vector/tensor insights.

### Route 7 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â Total MCSP through simple extensions

The 2026 simple-extension work identifies multiplexer as a plausible structural target for ETH-hardness of total MCSP. Characterizing optimal MUX circuits or extending the solver to superconstant fanout is concrete and underexplored.

This would illuminate MCSP and concept invention, but ETH-hardness is conditional and does not itself prove \(P\ne NP\).

---

## 11. Concrete experimental program

The following experiments cannot prove the asymptotic theorem alone, but they can expose false conjectures quickly and generate precise candidates.

### 11.1 Exact cyclic-intersection synthesis

For very small meta-input lengths:

1. enumerate the Gap-MCSP promise domain;
2. synthesize minimum cyclic AND constructions, with OR operations free;
3. compare acyclic intersection complexity and cyclic cover complexity;
4. compare with random targets, equality/inequality graphs, parity, and inner product.

This directly measures whether cycles collapse the proposed \(\Phi\) invariant on the target instances.

### 11.2 Column generation for semi-filter covers

Formulate cover complexity as set cover and its LP dual as a fractional semi-filter packing. Because explicit enumeration is enormous, alternate between:

* a restricted master LP over discovered semi-filters;
* a separation oracle searching for a pair that covers too much current mass;
* a witness oracle searching for an uncovered semi-filter.

Inspect learned dual weights for symmetries or global consistency patterns that could be stated asymptotically.

### 11.3 Tensor contraction diagnostics

For the same instances, test:

* treewidth before and after extension variables;
* Fourier/Hadamard and holographic basis changes;
* low-rank and matrix-product-state approximations;
* exact zero preservation under truncation.

The objective is not to infer a general lower bound from one parameter. It is to find which transformations repeatedly destroy local hardness and which residual obstruction survives them all.

### 11.4 Proof-generator curriculum

Train and evaluate on the following sequence:

1. ordinary PHP;
2. parity/Tseitin with an explicit Gaussian-elimination tool;
3. circuit-range formulas \(\tau_b(G)\) with known in-range and out-of-range targets;
4. low-degree XORÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“AND avoidance instances;
5. truth-table-generator fibers for tiny MCSP.

Measure not only proof length, but whether intermediate states predict:

* a preimage when one exists;
* an algebraic obstruction when none exists;
* a useful extension variable;
* an uncovered semi-filter or fractional-cover certificate.

### 11.5 Falsification suite

Every proposed invariant should be tested against:

* XOR systems solvable by Gaussian elimination;
* formulas simplified by one extension variable;
* high-rank functions with tiny ordinary circuits;
* high-treewidth instances with easy algebraic structure;
* unique-SAT instances;
* circuits with arbitrary low-fan-in oracle gates;
* cyclic constructions and unrestricted fanout.

An invariant that fails any one test may still prove a restricted result, but it cannot support the projectÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s unrestricted claim.

---

## 12. Corrections to earlier project claims

1. **Resolution:** Exponential resolution lower bounds do not imply exponential lower bounds for all SAT algorithms.
2. **Pigeonhole and Extended Resolution:** CookÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s classical construction gives polynomial-size Extended Resolution proofs; the safely supported scale is \(O(n^4)\), not a generic \(O(n^3)\) ER theorem.
3. **DRAT/PR versus ER:** Modern cubic-scale proof constructions in stronger practical systems should not be silently relabeled as the classical ER bound.
4. **Extended Frege/ER:** No universal polynomial bound is known; proving superpolynomial lower bounds remains open.
5. **MCSP:** Unrestricted total MCSP is not known NP-hard under ordinary deterministic many-one reductions.
6. **IPS:** Superpolynomial IPS lower bounds imply major algebraic circuit lower bounds, not a known direct proof of \(P\ne NP\).
7. **Neural experiments:** High accuracy on controlled proof families and improvements over greedy search are empirical results, not asymptotic proof-complexity theorems.
8. **Tensor/vector rank:** Lower bounds for one chosen format do not establish an unrestricted exact-compression lower bound.
9. **Canonical programs:** Existence of a shortest or best-scoring program does not imply efficient search or compositional optimality.
10. **Fusion complexity:** The unrestricted form is not a newly escaped measure; it is closely aligned with classical fusion/discrete/cyclic circuit complexity.

---

## 13. What a genuine completion would now look like

There are two clean, mutually opposite completion paths.

### To prove \(P=NP\)

Give a uniform polynomial-time operation on the exact factored tensor representation that decides whether \(\psi_C=0\), or finds a point with \(E_F(p)<1\) whenever one exists. By Theorems 1ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Å“3, either operation immediately yields a polynomial SAT algorithm and a witness-recovery procedure.

The construction itself is complete. The only missing theorem is the semantic algorithm.

### To prove \(P\ne NP\)

The strongest route isolated here is to prove the Promise Fusion Magnification LemmaÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â€žÂ¢s premise: a slightly superlinear, nonlocal semi-filter cover lower bound for Gap-MCSP. Alternatively, construct a single-valued NP-level missing output for the truth-table generator at superpolynomial size.

Both targets are crisp enough to be falsified, experimented on, and compared against known barriers. Neither is currently proved.

---

## 14. Bottom line

The backward-preimage vector **can always be kept in polynomial-size exact factored form**. That point is settled affirmatively by the gate-tensor construction.

What cannot currently be added is a polynomial-time exact semantic operation that tells whether the factor represents anything. Adding that operation is equivalent to solving SAT in polynomial time. The vector direction construction sharpens this further: even polynomial dimension, polynomial precision, and a constant angular gap do not remove the global nonconvex search.

The most important project-wide insight is that SAT inversion, MCSP, Kt minimization, learning, proof search, Berry diagonalization, fusion, and Range Avoidance are all operations on the fibers of succinct maps. The most promising new bridge is:
\[
\text{Gap-MCSP partial graph}
\xrightarrow{\text{fusion cover lower bound}}
\text{slightly superlinear circuit lower bound}
\xrightarrow{\text{hardness magnification}}
NP\not\subseteq P/\mathrm{poly}
\xrightarrow{}
P\ne NP.
\]

The missing link is no longer vague. It is an explicit nonlocal avoidance/cover witness strong enough to survive cyclic constructions, extensions, basis changes, and local oracle gates. No such witness is presently known.

---

## 12. New structural obstruction: the graph relaxation collapses

This section records a formal falsification test for the two-dimensional route in Section 7.2. The conclusion is a limitation of that route, not a circuit lower bound.

### Proposition 12.1 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â the promise graph is a rectangle

Let \(N=2^n\) be the truth-table length and split each \(N\)-bit truth table according to the first input variable, so an input is a pair \((x,y)\) of truth tables on \(n-1\) variables. Let
\[
Y=\{z: \operatorname{CC}_n(z)\le s_1\},\qquad
Z=\{z: \operatorname{CC}_n(z)\ge s_2\},\qquad
\Gamma=Y\cup Z.
\]
Assume \(2s_1+O(1)<s_2\), as holds for \(s_1=2^{\beta n}/(cn)\), \(s_2=2^{\beta n}\), fixed \(c>0\), and sufficiently large \(n\). If \(L\) and \(R\) are the left and right projections of \(Y\), then
\[
Y=(L\times R)\cap\Gamma.
\]

#### Proof

If \((x,y)\in Y\), restricting a size-\(s_1\) circuit to either value of the first variable computes each cofactor with size at most \(s_1+O(1)\), so \(x\in L\) and \(y\in R\). Conversely, take any \(x\in L\) and \(y\in R\). By definition, each is a cofactor of some size-\(s_1\) truth table, hence each has circuit size at most \(s_1+O(1)\). A selector on the first variable combines circuits for \(x\) and \(y\) using at most \(2s_1+O(1)<s_2\) gates. Thus their concatenation is not in \(Z\). If it is in the promise domain \(\Gamma\), it must therefore be in \(Y\). This proves the identity.

### Corollary 12.2 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â the proposed graph-cover lower bound is impossible

In the graph model with an unrestricted row star and column star for each vertex as generators, the identity above gives
\[
Y=\Bigl(\bigcup_{u\in L}R_u\Bigr)\cap\Bigl(\bigcup_{v\in R}C_v\Bigr)\quad\text{on }\Gamma.
\]
This uses one intersection, so \(D_{\cap}^{\rm graph}(Y)\le1\), and the fusion inequality gives \(\rho_{\rm graph}(Y)\le1\). Consequently this graph encoding cannot supply the \(N^{1+\varepsilon}\) cover lower bound proposed in Section 7.2.

The unrestricted row/column generators hide the hard part: the sets \(L,R\) are circuit-size classes on half truth tables, and their unions are free in the graph model. This does **not** give a small circuit over the original \(2n\) bit-literal generators. The direct promise fusion-cover lower bound over those bit literals remains open; the graph transference route is only a sufficient proxy and is too weak here.

The formal semi-filter and cover definitions used above are those of [Cavalar and Oliveira, *Boolean Circuit Complexity and Two-Dimensional Cover Problems*](https://arxiv.org/html/2503.14117v1). The parameter implication being targeted comes from [Oliveira, Pich, and Santhanam, *Hardness Magnification Near State-of-the-Art Lower Bounds*](https://theoryofcomputing.org/articles/v017a011/). This proposition is an elementary cofactor argument developed in this audit; no claim is made that it resolves the underlying circuit lower-bound problem.

### Proposition 12.3 ÃƒÂ¢Ã¢â€šÂ¬Ã¢â‚¬Â one pair covers all canonical bit-literal filters

For \(a\in Y\), define its canonical semi-filter by
\[
\mathcal F_a^{\rm can}=\{W\subseteq U:\exists i\in[N],\ B_{i,a_i}\cap U\subseteq W\}.
\]
For the magnification parameters with fixed \(0<\beta<1\), the family of all these canonical filters has cover number at most one.

#### Proof

The number of circuits of size at most \(s\) on \(n\) inputs is at most \(2^{O(s\log(n+s))}\). The same count ensures every one-coordinate slice of \(U\) is nonempty.
